FAQ & Troubleshooting

Stelo CGM

Stelo is designed for adults (18 years and older) who are not on insulin therapy. If you are pregnant or at risk of hypoglycemia (low blood sugar), please consult your healthcare provider before using Stelo.

Insert the biosensor on the back of your upper arm. This is the recommended placement for accurate readings. Each time you start a new biosensor session, choose a slightly different spot on your arm to allow previous sites to recover and to reduce the risk of skin irritation.

Inserting the Stelo biosensor is designed to be simple and essentially painless. Most people experience little to no discomfort during insertion.

Follow these steps for a successful insertion:

  • Choose a flat, clean, dry spot on the back of your upper arm. The skin should have some fat layer underneath.
  • Avoid areas with hair — if needed, shave with electric clippers (not a razor) first.
  • If you have adhesive residue from a previous biosensor, remove it before inserting a new one. A body oil or adhesive remover (such as Uni-solve or Tac Away) can help.
  • Press the applicator firmly against your skin until the clear safety guard is pushed in. This activates the button on the side.
  • Press the button to insert the biosensor.
  • Apply the overpatch (included in the box) immediately. Rub around the entire patch three times, smooth out any wrinkles, and press gently on top of the sensor for 10 seconds.

If you find it difficult to reach or position the applicator, ask a friend or family member.

The button will not activate until you press the applicator firmly enough against your skin to push in the clear safety guard. Press harder against your skin, and the button should become pressable. Then push it to insert the biosensor.

The Stelo biosensor connects to your smartphone via the Stelo app which you can then pair to the SynchNeuro app.

The serial number is printed on the side of the Stelo applicator and on the outside of the product box. When you insert your biosensor, take a photo of the serial number for your records — you may need it if you contact study support or report a product issue. The serial number begins after the characters “(21)” on the label.

To use Stelo, your primary residence must be in the US, and your Stelo app and account must be created within the US. Once you’re set up, you can use your Stelo during international travels.

Each Stelo biosensor is designed for a single session of up to 15 days.

Keeping your biosensor securely attached for the full session is important for complete data collection. Here are the most effective steps:

Before insertion:

  • Store unused biosensors at room temperature (36– 86°F) in a dry place.
  • Make sure the insertion site is completely flat, clean, and dry.
  • Remove any adhesive residue from previous sensors.
  • Avoid areas with significant hair growth.

After insertion:

  • Rub around the entire patch three times immediately after applying. Smooth out any wrinkles.
  • Keep the patch as dry and sweat-free as possible during the first 12 hours — this helps the adhesive bond well.
  • When the patch gets wet, gently pat it dry as soon as you can.
  • If any edge of the patch starts to peel, trim the lifted part and apply medical tape or an additional overpatch over the edges.
  • Avoid sleeping directly on the biosensor if possible.

Yes. The Stelo biosensor is water-resistant and can be worn in the shower or while swimming. After getting the patch wet, gently pat it dry. During the first 12 hours after insertion, try to keep the patch as dry as possible to allow the adhesive to bond fully.

Yes. If you find the biosensor is beginning to peel, you can apply additional adhesive patches or medical tape over or around the sensor. Some options that work well include:

  • Cloth-like patches (such as GrifGrips or Simpatch) — flexible, breathable, and good in wet conditions.
  • Hypafix — a fabric-like tape that can be cut to size.
  • Transpore Surgical Tape — a clear, porous medical tape, best used to reinforce peeling edges.

If using additional adhesive, do not apply liquid adhesives directly over the biosensor itself, as this can affect accuracy. Instead, apply them around the sensor (forming an oval or picture frame shape) and on the overpatch fabric.

If you cannot tolerate additional adhesive, a loose, non-compressive wrap (such as Coban or Ace bandage) can be used to keep the sensor in place, particularly during physical activity. Remove wraps overnight to avoid compressing the tissue.

This information is based on clinical guidance from independent researchers and has not been formally tested by Dexcom. For detailed insertion instructions, refer to the Stelo User Guide.

The Stelo biosensor patch uses a medical-grade, pressure-sensitive, acrylic-based adhesive. It has passed irritation testing and is latex-free.

If your phone was out of range of your biosensor for a period of time (or if Bluetooth was turned off), you may see a gap in your trend graph. This is normal and not a cause for concern. When your phone and biosensor are back within range (within about 20 feet / 6 meters), the missing data will automatically fill in. The biosensor can store up to 24 hours of readings while offline.

This alert means your biosensor is temporarily unable to determine your glucose reading. This is a known issue that can occur, particularly during the first day of a session, but it can happen at any time. In most cases, it resolves on its own within 3 hours.

While you see this alert, your glucose readings will be paused. Check your biosensor and, if available, tap Help in the Stelo app for additional guidance.

This means your biosensor has stopped reading before the expected 15-day session is complete. This can happen if the sensor is knocked off or if it becomes unable to detect your glucose. To reduce the chance of this happening:

  • Apply the overpatch immediately after insertion and make sure it is smooth and firmly attached.
  • Keep the patch dry and sweat-free for the first 12 hours.
  • Pat the patch dry after it gets wet.
  • If edges peel, trim them and apply medical tape.
  • Avoid placing or sleeping on the biosensor.

If your session ends early, submit a sensor issue report in the SynchNeuro app. They can advise on next steps and whether a replacement biosensor should be used.

This alert appears if you try to start a new session using a biosensor that has already been used or has expired. Each biosensor can only be used once. If you see this message, remove the biosensor and submit a sensor issue report in the SynchNeuro app.

Live agents are available during standard business hours.

For study support, refer to the SynchNeuro app to submit a report.

The Stelo biosensor uses Bluetooth to send glucose readings to the Stelo app on your phone which are then forwarded to the SynchNeuro app once setup is complete. Your phone’s Bluetooth must be turned on at all times. The biosensor connects, sends a reading, and disconnects every 5 minutes — so it is normal for the Bluetooth connection to show as “Not Connected” most of the time. This is expected behavior.

Each biosensor you use is saved as a separate device in your phone’s Bluetooth list. Before inserting a new biosensor, it is good practice to remove old entries.

iPhone:

  • Go to Settings \> Bluetooth.
  • Find used biosensors (they start with “DXCM”).
  • Tap the “i” icon next to a used biosensor.
  • Tap Forget This Device.

Android:

  • Go to Settings \> Connections \> Bluetooth.
  • Find used biosensors starting with “DXCM” in the paired devices list.
  • Tap Settings next to a used sensor.
  • Tap Unpair.

If you need to remove old biosensors while wearing your current one: all biosensors will appear as “Not Connected” in the list. To identify your current biosensor, watch the list for up to 5 minutes — the active one will briefly switch to “Connected” when it transmits a reading. If you accidentally remove the current biosensor, it will reconnect automatically within 15 minutes.

On iOS 18 and later, Apple introduced a privacy notification that appears when pairing any Bluetooth device, informing you that your location could theoretically be tracked. You must tap “Allow” for the biosensor to pair successfully. The Stelo app does not track your location based on Bluetooth information.

Dexcom continually tests the latest smartphones and Operating Systems (OS). The following is the range of operating systems that have completed testing and proven to be fully functional with Stelo. Stelo will still work with any OS that meets the minimum requirements.

Highest supported OS:

  • iOS 26.2.1
  • Android 16

Minimum required OS:

  • iOS 18.6
  • Android 13

Smartwatch information

You can use your smartwatch as an extension of this app. To get sensor readings on your smartwatch, it must be connected to and within range of your phone.

Use any smartwatch that meets the minimum operating system (OS) requirements.

  • Apple Watch OS 11.6

The Stelo biosensor measures the glucose in the fluid just under your skin (called interstitial fluid). It provides a reading approximately every 5 minutes. A reading within your target range indicates your glucose is at a healthy level for you at that moment.

It is normal for your Stelo biosensor reading and a fingerstick blood glucose meter (BGM) reading to show different numbers. Here is why:

  • The Stelo measures glucose in the fluid under your skin, while a BGM measures glucose in your blood. These naturally differ slightly.
  • On the first day of a new biosensor session, differences between your BGM and Stelo may be greater. Readings generally become more consistent after the first 24 hours.
  • If something is pressing on the biosensor (such as lying on it), this can affect readings. Relieve the pressure and the numbers should stabilize.
  • If your glucose is changing rapidly, there may be a lag between the biosensor and a blood measurement. Numbers will converge as glucose stabilizes.
  • Make sure your test strips are stored correctly and are not expired, and that your hands are thoroughly washed with soap and water before using a BGM.

Stelo glucose data has been shown to be highly accurate when compared to laboratory blood glucose standards.

Sometimes how you feel does not match the number shown in the app. If you have symptoms of low or high glucose but your readings look normal, or your readings are unexpected, trust your symptoms. If you need to check your glucose in the moment, a fingerstick blood glucose meter can be used as a backup.

Glucose levels can fall below 70 mg/dL (a level called hypoglycemia) for a variety of everyday reasons, including skipping a meal, intense physical activity, or alcohol consumption. This is not always a cause for alarm. The Stelo app will flag patterns of low glucose and provide insights to help you understand what may be causing them.

If your glucose levels are consistently below your target range, consult your healthcare provider. If you have symptoms that concern you (such as feeling shaky, confused, or faint), take appropriate action immediately and contact a healthcare professional.

A single high glucose reading does not mean you have diabetes. Stress, certain foods, physical activity, and other factors can temporarily raise glucose levels. Stelo helps you notice patterns over time, not individual data points in isolation.

If you notice that your glucose levels are consistently above your target range, discuss this with your healthcare provider. They can help interpret the patterns in the context of your overall health.

The Stelo app sets a default target range based on the health status you select during setup:

  • 70–180 mg/dL — recommended for people with type 2 diabetes who do not use insulin.
  • 70–140 mg/dL — recommended for people with prediabetes or without diabetes.

You can customize your target range in the Stelo app at any time (Settings \> Target Range) if your healthcare provider recommends a different range for you.

The 70-250 mg/dL trend graph in the Stelo app helps to provide more focus on the recommended target ranges to stay within.

For an extended view of your glucose data, including when your glucose went below 70 mg/dL and/or above 250 mg/dL, visit Dexcom Clarity: clarity.dexcom.com.

Some people are sensitive to the biosensor adhesive. If you notice itching, burning, or a rash at the sensor site:

  • Please submit a symptom report in the SynchNeuro app
  • Do not apply a new biosensor to the same spot twice in a row. Rotate insertion sites each session.
  • Consider moisturizing your skin between biosensor sessions to prevent dryness — but do not apply moisturizer on the day of insertion, and do not get it on the sensor or patch.
  • Contact your healthcare provider if you experience significant irritation (notable itching, burning, or rash).

Yes. The Stelo biosensor is FDA-cleared and available over the counter for adults not on insulin. Insertion is minimally invasive and designed to be painless for most people.

If you have any health concerns about using a CGM during the study, discuss them with your healthcare provider.

If you experience any unexpected physical symptoms (such as pain, unusual skin reaction, or suspected infection) at or near the biosensor site:

  • Contact your healthcare provider or seek medical attention.
  • Submit a report in the SynchNeuro app so the study coordinators can document the issue and provide support.

Polar Verity Sense

Before your first use, we recommend charging the sensor fully. If the battery is completely empty, it may take a few minutes for charging to begin.The Polar Verity Sense uses Bluetooth to send heart rate data to your smartphone. You will pair it with the SynchNeuro app during your study onboarding. To pair:

  • Make sure Bluetooth is turned on on your phone.
  • Follow the pairing instructions in the SynchNeuro app.

Once paired, the app will display your sensor as connected.

The Polar Verity Sense uses Bluetooth to stream heart rate data to your smartphone. You can optionally use the Polar Flow app to view your heart rate and other data from the Polar Verity Sense sensor:

  • Download the Polar Flow app from the App Store or Google Play.
  • Make sure Bluetooth is turned on on your phone.
  • Put on the sensor so it is in contact with your skin — it will not transmit if not worn.
  • Follow the pairing instructions in the Polar Flow app.

Once paired, the app will display the Verity Sense as connected. You can find your sensor’s device ID in the Devices menu within the Polar Flow app, or printed directly on the sensor.

The Verity Sense supports three operating modes:

  • Heart rate mode: Streams heart rate in real time via Bluetooth and ANT+ to a compatible app or device.
  • Recording mode: Stores training sessions in the sensor’s internal memory (up to 600 hours), which can be synced to the Polar Flow app later.
  • Swimming mode: Automatically records heart rate, distance, and pace during pool swimming sessions using an integrated gyroscope to detect turns.

The serial number and device ID are printed on the sensor itself. You can also find the device ID in the Devices menu of the Polar Flow app, or in the Products menu of the Polar Flow web service.

Yes. The Verity Sense can stream heart rate simultaneously to up to two different Bluetooth devices. It is also compatible with ANT+ devices. The sensor works with dozens of leading fitness apps in addition to Polar Flow, including any app that supports standard Bluetooth heart rate profiles.

The Verity Sense is an optical sensor worn on the arm or temple. Wearing it correctly is essential for accurate readings.

For general use on the arm (forearm or upper arm):

  • Wear the sensor snugly on your forearm or upper arm — not on your wrist.
  • The sensor lens must be in firm, constant contact with your skin. Movement between the sensor and skin will reduce accuracy.
  • The armband should feel secure but not uncomfortably tight.

For pool swimming:

  • Attach the Verity Sense to your swimming goggle strap using the included swimming goggle strap clip.
  • Position the sensor against your temple, as far toward the hairline as possible, without getting hair between the sensor and your skin.

*Note: Tattoos on the skin beneath the sensor can interfere with the optical reading. If possible, avoid placing the sensor directly on tattooed areas.*

No. For heart rate measurement, the Verity Sense is intended to be worn on the forearm or upper arm, not the wrist. In swimming mode, it is worn against the temple via the goggle strap clip.

Yes. The Polar Verity Sense is intended to be worn continuously throughout the duration of the 14-day study.

Some people may experience skin irritation from the sensor or armband materials. If you notice a rash, redness, or significant discomfort:

  • Stop wearing the sensor, and please submit a symptom report under the *Report a Symptom* option in the SynchNeuro app.
  • Consult your physician if you have a known allergy to any of the sensor’s materials. Material details are available in the Polar Verity Sense User Manual.

The Verity Sense has a built-in rechargeable battery. To charge:

  • Place the Verity Sense into the USB charging adapter included in the package.
  • Orient the sensor with the lens facing upward so the contacts on the sensor and the adapter meet correctly.
  • Connect the USB adapter to a USB power source (laptop, wall adapter, etc.).

The battery provides up to 20 hours of use on a full charge.

*Note: If the battery is completely empty, it may take several minutes before charging begins. If the sensor still does not charge after 10 minutes, try repositioning it in the USB adapter.*

Signs that the battery may be running low include:

  • The app is not showing a heart rate reading.
  • Heart rate readings appear abnormal or stop updating.
  • The sensor disconnects from the app more easily than usual.

You can also check the current battery level in the Devices menu of the Polar Flow app.

Rechargeable batteries degrade over time, and capacity will gradually decrease with regular use. Battery lifespan depends on factors including how often the device is charged, storage conditions, and usage patterns. For best battery health, avoid storing the device fully discharged for extended periods.

Keeping the sensor clean ensures both accurate readings and comfort over extended wear.

After removing the sensor:

  • Rinse the sensor under lukewarm running tap water. The Verity Sense is water-resistant and safe to rinse without removing any components.
  • Clean with a mild soap and water solution when needed.
  • Dry with a soft towel before storing.

What to avoid:

  • Never use alcohol, disinfectants, or abrasive materials (e.g., steel wool or rough scrubbing pads) on the sensor.
  • Do not put the sensor in a washing machine or dishwasher.
  • Avoid getting perfume, lotion, sunscreen, or insect repellent on the sensor surface.
  • Store the sensor in a cool, dry place.

*Note: The optical lens is sensitive to dirt and scratches. Even a small amount of residue on the lens can reduce measurement accuracy. Similarly, scratches scatter LED light in unintended directions and weaken the sensor’s performance. Handle the lens with care.*

The Polar Verity Sense is water-resistant to 50 meters making it well-suited for swimming, showering, and other water activities. You do not need to remove it for typical daily activities involving water.

Keep in mind:

  • Pool water with high chlorine content can affect the skin’s optical properties and may temporarily influence readings.
  • After swimming in heavily chlorinated pool water or seawater, rinse the sensor promptly.
  • The 50 m rating refers to static water pressure, not actual diving depth. The device is not intended for scuba diving or high-pressure water activities.

If your heart rate is not appearing in the app, work through these steps:

  • Make sure the sensor lens is clean and free of dirt or dried residue.
  • Confirm you are wearing the sensor correctly — it must be snug and the lens must be firmly against your skin.
  • Ensure Bluetooth is turned on on your phone and that the sensor is paired with the Polar Flow app.
  • Keep your phone in front of you (e.g., in a front pocket). Bluetooth does not transmit reliably through the human body.
  • Exit the app and restart it, then check if the sensor reconnects.
  • If the problem continues, try restarting your phone.
  • Check whether the battery needs charging

If you are having trouble pairing with either the SynchNeuro app or the Polar Flow app:

  • Make sure Bluetooth is enabled on your phone.
  • Make sure you are wearing the sensor (it needs to be in contact with skin to activate and transmit).
  • If the sensor appears in your phone’s Bluetooth device list but won’t connect, remove it from the list and try pairing again.
  • Restart your phone’s Bluetooth and attempt pairing again.

A factory reset can resolve persistent issues that other troubleshooting steps have not fixed. Note that resetting the sensor will erase all stored data, and you will need to set it up again.

To reset the Verity Sense, use the Polar Flow app. Refer to the instructions within the app or the user manual for the exact steps.

*Note: After a factory reset, any unsynced data stored in the sensor’s memory will be permanently lost. Make sure to sync before resetting if you want to preserve your data.*

Polar periodically releases firmware updates for the Verity Sense that improve performance and fix issues. Firmware updates are performed through the Polar Flow app.

The Flow app will notify you when a firmware update is available.

The Verity Sense transmits heart rate via both Bluetooth and ANT+. In open, unobstructed conditions, Bluetooth range is typically several meters. Walls, the human body, and other wireless devices can all reduce this range.

For best results, keep your phone within a few meters of your body and in a front pocket or accessible location.

ANT+ is a wireless transmission protocol similar to Bluetooth but does not require pairing — any compatible ANT+ receiver nearby can pick up the signal. The Verity Sense supports both Bluetooth and ANT+, making it compatible with a wide range of fitness apps and training devices, including GPS watches and cycling computers.

The Polar Verity Sense is a consumer optical heart rate sensor suitable for most adults. As with any activity monitoring device, there are a few things to be aware of:

  • Physical activity involves some inherent risk. If you have any health concerns (such as high blood pressure, a history of heart or breathing problems, or if you are pregnant), speak with your healthcare provider before participating in any physical activity during the study.
  • Medications for heart conditions, blood pressure, asthma, and some other conditions, as well as energy drinks, alcohol, and nicotine, can affect heart rate readings.
  • If you feel unexpected pain or excessive fatigue while exercising with the Verity Sense, stop the activity and rest.
  • If you experience any unusual symptoms while using the sensor, please report your symptom(s) in the SynchNeuro app.

People using pacemakers or other implanted electronic devices can generally use Polar products. No reports of interference have been documented in practice. However, because many different types of implanted devices exist, Polar cannot provide an official guarantee of compatibility for all devices.

If you have any concerns, please consult your physician or the manufacturer of your implanted device before using the Polar Verity Sense in the study.

EEG Sensor

The SynchNeuro sensor records electrical brain activity (EEG) while you wear it. The data collected during this study will be used for research and development purposes. The app does not currently display EEG derived health insights — that feature is being developed for future versions.

The sensor sits behind your left ear, resting on the hard bony bump directly behind your earlobe. It should hug the curve of your ear with the pointed tip facing away from your head.

No. The SynchNeuro system is not intended to diagnose, treat, or manage any medical condition. It is a research-grade wearable used in this study purely to collect data for developmental purposes.

Yes — for the best results during the 14-day study, please wear the device continuously, including during sleep. The sensor records automatically once applied, even if the app is closed.

No. You must remove the sensor pod from the patch electrode before showering, bathing, or swimming. Water will damage the device. The patch electrode can remain in place during showering or swimming.

⚠ Do NOT expose the sensor to water. Remove before showering, bathing, or swimming.

Remove earrings, glasses, and hats before applying the sensor. Once it is secured, you may put them back on, but be mindful not to disturb the patch placement.

Remove the sensor and patch immediately. Clean the area gently with mild soap and water. Do not reapply until the irritation has resolved, and submit a symptom report via the SynchNeuro app.

Every day. Gently remove the sensor pod from behind your ear (without peeling off the adhesive patch), place it in the charging case, and connect it to the USB cable. A full charge takes up to 2 hours.

The LED on the charging case will flash while charging. When charging is complete, the LED will stay solid (stops flashing).

Every 7 days, or sooner if it starts to peel away from your skin. Never reuse a patch once it has been removed.

If the patch begins to detach, apply a new one as soon as possible. Contact the study team if you need replacement patches.

Yes, recording continues in the background. Your phone should stay within approximately 10 meters (33 feet) of the sensor for data to sync — staying within 4 meters is ideal.
